I mean, I'm still teaching and meeting my basic responsibilities in the classroom—I'm getting through the day. But... I've definitely missed or pushed back a few planning deadlines. I just kind of avoid looking at them right now. And I've actually needed reminders from my colleagues lately for tasks that I used to complete really reliably. I just keep putting those things off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities that you typically find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'd say I still enjoy things. I mean... I still like reading, or just relaxing on the weekends. I haven't really noticed any change with that. It's just that I have a lot less time for those things right now because of all the extra school work. But when I do actually get a minute to myself to do something I like, I still enjoy it just fine.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or conversations with colleagues because of your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding people. I still talk to my colleagues during the day, you know, in the break room or between classes. I'm not hiding from them or anything. It's really just the paperwork and the planning tasks that I'm putting off, not the social part of the job.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say unusually tired. I mean, teaching is always a bit tiring by the end of the day, but my energy levels are pretty normal for me. I haven't noticed feeling physically drained or anything like that. It's really just the stress of the extra workload that's getting to me, not a lack of energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or things you usually find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: No, I think we just covered that a minute ago. Like I said, I still enjoy reading and relaxing when I have the time. My interest in those things hasn't dropped at all, I just have less free time to actually do them right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're worried about not doing them perfectly?\n\nPatient: No, it's not really about being perfect. I mean, I've been teaching long enough to know that things rarely go perfectly anyway. It's... it's just the sheer volume of it right now. I'm putting the paperwork off because the extra workload just feels overwhelming to even start, not because I'm worried about getting every little detail right.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to function in your daily life or at work because of these feelings of being overwhelmed and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I'm still teaching and meeting my basic responsibilities. The classroom is fine. But... yes, it has gotten a bit harder. I've actually missed or had to push back some of my planning deadlines lately. And I've even needed reminders from my colleagues for tasks I used to just complete reliably on my own. So, I'm still doing my job, but I'm definitely letting some of those administrative things slip.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my sleep has been pretty normal. I'm usually tired enough by the end of the day that I just fall right to sleep, so... no, I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite is about the same. I'm still eating normally. I haven't noticed any difference there either.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant changes or stressful events in your life recently, outside of the new workload at school?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the changes at work. My personal life has been pretty quiet and steady, so... no, there hasn't been anything else going on. Just the new curriculum and the extra meetings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about five months now. It really started right around midyear, when the district assigned me that new rollout role.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're afraid of not being able to handle them or fear that you might fail?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's that. I'm not really putting things off because I'm afraid of failing. I know how to do my job, it's just... well, it's just a lot of extra work to get through right now. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm scared I can't handle it.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're worried about what others might think of your performance?\n\nPatient: No, it's not that either. I mean, I've been teaching for a long time, so I'm not really concerned about what the principal or the other teachers think of me. It really just comes back to the sheer volume of it all. I just put things off because it's exhausting to even look at the pile, not because I'm worried about anyone judging my performance.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just don't feel like myself right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't really call it anxiety, no. I don't feel panicked or... nervous, exactly. It's more just that wound-up feeling I mentioned, just being really stressed and overwhelmed by all the extra work I have to get done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really sad. I mean, I haven't been crying or feeling hopeless or anything like that. It's really just that discouraged feeling I mentioned before, mostly because of the workload. I wouldn't say I'm down... just very frustrated and stretched too thin.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain tasks or situations because of this feeling of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I am. I mean, I'm still teaching and meeting my basic responsibilities in the classroom—I'm getting through the day. But... I've definitely missed or pushed back a few planning deadlines. I just kind of avoid looking at them right now. And I've actually needed reminders from my colleagues lately for tasks that I used to complete really reliably. I just keep putting those things off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying activities that you typically find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'd say I still enjoy things. I mean... I still like reading, or just relaxing on the weekends. I haven't really noticed any change with that. It's just that I have a lot less time for those things right now because of all the extra school work. But when I do actually get a minute to myself to do something I like, I still enjoy it just fine.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or conversations with colleagues because of your current situation?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding people. I still talk to my colleagues during the day, you know, in the break room or between classes. I'm not hiding from them or anything. It's really just the paperwork and the planning tasks that I'm putting off, not the social part of the job.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say unusually tired. I mean, teaching is always a bit tiring by the end of the day, but my energy levels are pretty normal for me. I haven't noticed feeling physically drained or anything like that. It's really just the stress of the extra workload that's getting to me, not a lack of energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities or things you usually find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: No, I think we just covered that a minute ago. Like I said, I still enjoy reading and relaxing when I have the time. My interest in those things hasn't dropped at all, I just have less free time to actually do them right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're worried about not doing them perfectly?\n\nPatient: No, it's not really about being perfect. I mean, I've been teaching long enough to know that things rarely go perfectly anyway. It's... it's just the sheer volume of it right now. I'm putting the paperwork off because the extra workload just feels overwhelming to even start, not because I'm worried about getting every little detail right.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to function in your daily life or at work because of these feelings of being overwhelmed and discouraged?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I'm still teaching and meeting my basic responsibilities. The classroom is fine. But... yes, it has gotten a bit harder. I've actually missed or had to push back some of my planning deadlines lately. And I've even needed reminders from my colleagues for tasks I used to just complete reliably on my own. So, I'm still doing my job, but I'm definitely letting some of those administrative things slip.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my sleep has been pretty normal. I'm usually tired enough by the end of the day that I just fall right to sleep, so... no, I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite is about the same. I'm still eating normally. I haven't noticed any difference there either.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant changes or stressful events in your life recently, outside of the new workload at school?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just the changes at work. My personal life has been pretty quiet and steady, so... no, there hasn't been anything else going on. Just the new curriculum and the extra meetings.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about five months now. It really started right around midyear, when the district assigned me that new rollout role.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're afraid of not being able to handle them or fear that you might fail?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's that. I'm not really putting things off because I'm afraid of failing. I know how to do my job, it's just... well, it's just a lot of extra work to get through right now. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm scared I can't handle it.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're worried about what others might think of your performance?\n\nPatient: No, it's not that either. I mean, I've been teaching for a long time, so I'm not really concerned about what the principal or the other teachers think of me. It really just comes back to the sheer volume of it all. I just put things off because it's exhausting to even look at the pile, not because I'm worried about anyone judging my performance.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to cope with the demands of your job or daily life because of these feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I'm completely unable to cope. I mean, I'm still in the classroom teaching and meeting my basic responsibilities. The kids are getting what they need. \n\nBut... I have definitely missed a few planning deadlines lately, or had to push them back. And I've actually needed reminders from my colleagues for tasks that I used to just complete reliably on my own. So, I'm still functioning, it's just that I'm dropping the ball a bit on the organizational side of things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

{"intent": "determine presence of avoidance due to fear of failure", "question": "Are you putting off tasks because you're afraid you might not do them well enough?"}

I have definitely missed a few planning deadlines lately, or had to push them back. And I've actually needed reminders from my colleagues for tasks that I used to just complete reliably on my own. So, I'm still functioning, it's just that I'm dropping the ball a bit on the organizational side of things.\n\nDoctor: Are you putting off tasks because you're afraid you might not do them well enough?\n\nPatient: No, it's not a perfectionism thing.
